It's actually been the exact opposite—I feel constantly revved up, wired, and completely unable to slow myself down.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through, such as spending money, blurting out answers, or interrupting others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been incredibly impulsive, like booking expensive event venues on my personal credit card and firing off unapproved, late-night emails to executives, which has made my wife absolutely furious about the money I've burned. I also catch myself talking nonstop and jumping from idea to idea so fast that others can't get a word in. I just feel this constant pressure to keep talking, even when I see their eyes glazing over.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulties with your relationships or work performance as a result of your impulsivity and constant need for activity, and if so, can you give me some specific examples?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has severely damaged both. At work, I was actually pulled off my flagship restructuring project because my team lost trust in me after I bypassed approvals and booked expensive venues on my personal card. At home, my wife is absolutely furious about the money I've burned through and how unreliable and wired I've become.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me at what age you first started experiencing these symptoms of excessive activity, impulsivity, and distractibility, and have they been present consistently throughout your life or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: These symptoms are actually very recent and not something I've dealt with my whole life—they only started about a month ago, ramping up as my big restructuring project took off. Normally, I'm known for my disciplined creativity, so this sudden shift to being so revved up and impulsive is a completely new and alarming change for me at 44.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about a month ago, but can you tell me if you've had any similar episodes in the past, even if they were less severe, or if this is completely new for you?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any episodes like this in the past, even milder ones. This level of being completely revved up and out of control is entirely new to me. Before this past month, I was always very disciplined and steady with my work and life.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant mood changes, such as feeling extremely euphoric or irritable, alongside your increased energy and activity levels?\n\nPatient: Yes, my mood has been swinging all over the place lately. I'll go from feeling on top of the world, like I'm a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to snapping at my team over tiny delays. If they push back at all, I feel this intense, white-hot anger that is completely out of proportion and hard to control.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe the nature of your elevated mood - is it more of an expansive, grandiose feeling where you feel invincible, or is it a more irritable, restless state?\n\nPatient: I actually experience both of those states, swinging from feeling on top of the world like a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to feeling extremely irritable. When my team pushes back, I snap over minor delays and feel this intense, white-hot anger that is totally out of proportion. Alongside that, my body constantly feels physically revved up and restless, like I can't even sit still on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when these feelings of being 'on top of the world' or 'invincible' occur - are they constant, or do they come and go, and are they related to your excessive activity and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: These 'on top of the world' feelings have been happening for about a month now, ramping up with my project, but they aren't constant since I swing between feeling like a visionary and snapping at my team with intense anger. They are directly tied to my non-stop activity and risky behaviors. When I'm in that high state, I feel driven by a motor to start endless projects and make impulsive, reckless decisions because everything feels so urgent and brilliant in the moment."

At home, my wife is absolutely furious about the money I've burned through and how unreliable and wired I've become.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me at what age you first started experiencing these symptoms of excessive activity, impulsivity, and distractibility, and have they been present consistently throughout your life or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: These symptoms are actually very recent and not something I've dealt with my whole life—they only started about a month ago, ramping up as my big restructuring project took off. Normally, I'm known for my disciplined creativity, so this sudden shift to being so revved up and impulsive is a completely new and alarming change for me at 44.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about a month ago, but can you tell me if you've had any similar episodes in the past, even if they were less severe, or if this is completely new for you?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any episodes like this in the past, even milder ones. This level of being completely revved up and out of control is entirely new to me. Before this past month, I was always very disciplined and steady with my work and life.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant mood changes, such as feeling extremely euphoric or irritable, alongside your increased energy and activity levels?\n\nPatient: Yes, my mood has been swinging all over the place lately. I'll go from feeling on top of the world, like I'm a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to snapping at my team over tiny delays. If they push back at all, I feel this intense, white-hot anger that is completely out of proportion and hard to control.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe the nature of your elevated mood - is it more of an expansive, grandiose feeling where you feel invincible, or is it a more irritable, restless state?\n\nPatient: I actually experience both of those states, swinging from feeling on top of the world like a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to feeling extremely irritable. When my team pushes back, I snap over minor delays and feel this intense, white-hot anger that is totally out of proportion. Alongside that, my body constantly feels physically revved up and restless, like I can't even sit still on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when these feelings of being 'on top of the world' or 'invincible' occur - are they constant, or do they come and go, and are they related to your excessive activity and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: These 'on top of the world' feelings have been happening for about a month now, ramping up with my project, but they aren't constant since I swing between feeling like a visionary and snapping at my team with intense anger. They are directly tied to my non-stop activity and risky behaviors. When I'm in that high state, I feel driven by a motor to start endless projects and make impulsive, reckless decisions because everything feels so urgent and brilliant in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your mood and energy levels returned to normal, even briefly, since these symptoms started about a month ago, or have they been consistently elevated and unstable?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any moments where things went back to normal; it has been consistently elevated and unstable the entire time. For this past month, I've felt like I'm constantly being driven by a motor I can't switch off. There simply hasn't been a break or any period where I felt like my usual self."

I just feel this constant pressure to keep talking, even when I see their eyes glazing over.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulties with your relationships or work performance as a result of your impulsivity and constant need for activity, and if so, can you give me some specific examples?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has severely damaged both. At work, I was actually pulled off my flagship restructuring project because my team lost trust in me after I bypassed approvals and booked expensive venues on my personal card. At home, my wife is absolutely furious about the money I've burned through and how unreliable and wired I've become.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me at what age you first started experiencing these symptoms of excessive activity, impulsivity, and distractibility, and have they been present consistently throughout your life or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: These symptoms are actually very recent and not something I've dealt with my whole life—they only started about a month ago, ramping up as my big restructuring project took off. Normally, I'm known for my disciplined creativity, so this sudden shift to being so revved up and impulsive is a completely new and alarming change for me at 44.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about a month ago, but can you tell me if you've had any similar episodes in the past, even if they were less severe, or if this is completely new for you?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any episodes like this in the past, even milder ones. This level of being completely revved up and out of control is entirely new to me. Before this past month, I was always very disciplined and steady with my work and life.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant mood changes, such as feeling extremely euphoric or irritable, alongside your increased energy and activity levels?\n\nPatient: Yes, my mood has been swinging all over the place lately. I'll go from feeling on top of the world, like I'm a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to snapping at my team over tiny delays. If they push back at all, I feel this intense, white-hot anger that is completely out of proportion and hard to control.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe the nature of your elevated mood - is it more of an expansive, grandiose feeling where you feel invincible, or is it a more irritable, restless state?\n\nPatient: I actually experience both of those states, swinging from feeling on top of the world like a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to feeling extremely irritable. When my team pushes back, I snap over minor delays and feel this intense, white-hot anger that is totally out of proportion. Alongside that, my body constantly feels physically revved up and restless, like I can't even sit still on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when these feelings of being 'on top of the world' or 'invincible' occur - are they constant, or do they come and go, and are they related to your excessive activity and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: These 'on top of the world' feelings have been happening for about a month now, ramping up with my project, but they aren't constant since I swing between feeling like a visionary and snapping at my team with intense anger. They are directly tied to my non-stop activity and risky behaviors. When I'm in that high state, I feel driven by a motor to start endless projects and make impulsive, reckless decisions because everything feels so urgent and brilliant in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your mood and energy levels returned to normal, even briefly, since these symptoms started about a month ago, or have they been consistently elevated and unstable?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any moments where things went back to normal; it has been consistently elevated and unstable the entire time. For this past month, I've felt like I'm constantly being driven by a motor I can't switch off. There simply hasn't been a break or any period where I felt like my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if you have experienced any distinct periods of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted for at least one week, or if your mood has been fluctuating rapidly without any clear episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, this mix of feeling like a visionary on top of the world and experiencing that intense, white-hot irritability has been going on consistently for about a month now. It isn't just rapid, fleeting fluctuations; it has been a steady, ongoing state of instability ever since the restructuring project started."
  }

When my team pushes back, I snap over minor delays and feel this intense, white-hot anger that is totally out of proportion. Alongside that, my body constantly feels physically revved up and restless, like I can't even sit still on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when these feelings of being 'on top of the world' or 'invincible' occur - are they constant, or do they come and go, and are they related to your excessive activity and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: These 'on top of the world' feelings have been happening for about a month now, ramping up with my project, but they aren't constant since I swing between feeling like a visionary and snapping at my team with intense anger. They are directly tied to my non-stop activity and risky behaviors. For this past month, I've felt like I'm constantly being driven by a motor I can't switch off. There simply hasn't been a break or any period where I felt like my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if you have experienced any distinct periods of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted for at least one week, or if your mood has been fluctuating rapidly without any clear episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, this mix of feeling like a visionary on top of the world and experiencing that intense, white-hot irritability has been going on consistently for about a month now. It isn't just rapid, fleeting fluctuations; it has been a steady, ongoing state of instability ever since the restructuring project started.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if there was a specific week or longer period within the past month where you felt consistently elevated or irritable without any significant breaks, helping to differentiate between a manic, hypomanic, or mixed episode?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been the entire month without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring project started, I've been stuck in this constant cycle of feeling like a brilliant visionary one moment and then snapping with intense, white-hot anger the next. It has been a solid, continuous block of feeling completely revved up and unstable."

When I'm in that high state, I feel driven by a motor to start endless projects and make impulsive, reckless decisions because everything feels so urgent and brilliant in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your mood and energy levels returned to normal, even briefly, since these symptoms started about a month ago, or have they been consistently elevated and unstable?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any moments where things went back to normal; it has been consistently elevated and unstable the entire time. For this past month, I've felt like I'm constantly being driven by a motor I can't switch off. There simply hasn't been a break or any period where I felt like my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if you have experienced any distinct periods of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted for at least one week, or if your mood has been fluctuating rapidly without any clear episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, this mix of feeling like a visionary on top of the world and experiencing that intense, white-hot irritability has been going on consistently for about a month now. It isn't just rapid, fleeting fluctuations; it has been a steady, ongoing state of instability ever since the restructuring project started.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if there was a specific week or longer period within the past month where you felt consistently elevated or irritable without any significant breaks, helping to differentiate between a manic, hypomanic, or mixed episode?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been the entire month without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring project started, I've been stuck in this constant cycle of feeling like a brilliant visionary one moment and then snapping with intense, white-hot anger the next. It has been a solid, continuous block of feeling completely revved up and unstable.\n\nDoctor: You've described a month-long period of elevated mood, increased energy, and impulsivity without any breaks. Can you confirm if, at any point during this month, your symptoms were severe enough to cause significant impairment in your social or occupational functioning, or if they were more of a nuisance but still allowed you to function, albeit poorly?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has absolutely caused severe impairment, far beyond just being a nuisance. I was actually pulled off my flagship restructuring project at work because my team completely lost trust in my decisions. At home, my wife is furious about the money I've burned through and how unreliable and wired I've become."

What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: I’ve been taking on way too many projects at once and can’t seem to slow myself down at work or at home. It's gotten to the point where it's causing a lot of friction, and my sister strongly urged me to come get some help.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'can't seem to slow yourself down' - are you finding it hard to focus on one task at a time or feeling an overwhelming urge to keep taking on more?\n\nPatient: It’s definitely both. I feel this overwhelming, physical urge to keep taking on more, starting side projects in the middle of the night like I’m driven by a motor. But at the same time, I'm incredibly distractible and constantly lose my train of thought because every little sound or movement pulls my attention away.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of time where you felt extremely down, hopeless, or had very little energy, contrasting with these periods of high energy and excessive activity?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any periods of feeling down, hopeless, or low on energy. It's actually been the exact opposite—I feel constantly revved up, wired, and completely unable to slow myself down.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through, such as spending money, blurting out answers, or interrupting others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been incredibly impulsive, like booking expensive event venues on my personal credit card and firing off unapproved, late-night emails to executives, which has made my wife absolutely furious about the money I've burned. I also catch myself talking nonstop and jumping from idea to idea so fast that others can't get a word in. I just feel this constant pressure to keep talking, even when I see their eyes glazing over.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulties with your relationships or work performance as a result of your impulsivity and constant need for activity, and if so, can you give me some specific examples?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has severely damaged both. At work, I was actually pulled off my flagship restructuring project because my team lost trust in me after I bypassed approvals and booked expensive venues on my personal card. At home, my wife is absolutely furious about the money I've burned through and how unreliable and wired I've become.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me at what age you first started experiencing these symptoms of excessive activity, impulsivity, and distractibility, and have they been present consistently throughout your life or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: These symptoms are actually very recent and not something I've dealt with my whole life—they only started about a month ago, ramping up as my big restructuring project took off. Normally, I'm known for my disciplined creativity, so this sudden shift to being so revved up and impulsive is a completely new and alarming change for me at 44.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about a month ago, but can you tell me if you've had any similar episodes in the past, even if they were less severe, or if this is completely new for you?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any episodes like this in the past, even milder ones. This level of being completely revved up and out of control is entirely new to me. Before this past month, I was always very disciplined and steady with my work and life.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant mood changes, such as feeling extremely euphoric or irritable, alongside your increased energy and activity levels?\n\nPatient: Yes, my mood has been swinging all over the place lately. I'll go from feeling on top of the world, like I'm a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to snapping at my team over tiny delays. If they push back at all, I feel this intense, white-hot anger that is completely out of proportion and hard to control.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe the nature of your elevated mood - is it more of an expansive, grandiose feeling where you feel invincible, or is it a more irritable, restless state?\n\nPatient: I actually experience both of those states, swinging from feeling on top of the world like a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to feeling extremely irritable. When my team pushes back, I snap over minor delays and feel this intense, white-hot anger that is totally out of proportion. Alongside that, my body constantly feels physically revved up and restless, like I can't even sit still on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when these feelings of being 'on top of the world' or 'invincible' occur - are they constant, or do they come and go, and are they related to your excessive activity and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: These 'on top of the world' feelings have been happening for about a month now, ramping up with my project, but they aren't constant since I swing between feeling like a visionary and snapping at my team with intense anger. They are directly tied to my non-stop activity and risky behaviors. When I'm in that high state, I feel driven by a motor to start endless projects and make impulsive, reckless decisions because everything feels so urgent and brilliant in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your mood and energy levels returned to normal, even briefly, since these symptoms started about a month ago, or have they been consistently elevated and unstable?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any moments where things went back to normal; it has been consistently elevated and unstable the entire time. For this past month, I've felt like I'm constantly being driven by a motor I can't switch off. There simply hasn't been a break or any period where I felt like my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if you have experienced any distinct periods of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted for at least one week, or if your mood has been fluctuating rapidly without any clear episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, this mix of feeling like a visionary on top of the world and experiencing that intense, white-hot irritability has been going on consistently for about a month now. It isn't just rapid, fleeting fluctuations; it has been a steady, ongoing state of instability ever since the restructuring project started.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if there was a specific week or longer period within the past month where you felt consistently elevated or irritable without any significant breaks, helping to differentiate between a manic, hypomanic, or mixed episode?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been the entire month without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring project started, I've been stuck in this constant cycle of feeling like a brilliant visionary one moment and then snapping with intense, white-hot anger the next. It has been a solid, continuous block of feeling completely revved up and unstable.\n\nDoctor: You've described a month-long period of elevated mood, increased energy, and impulsivity without any breaks. Can you confirm if, at any point during this month, your symptoms were severe enough to cause significant impairment in your social or occupational functioning, or if they were more of a nuisance but still allowed you to function, albeit poorly?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has absolutely caused severe impairment, far beyond just being a nuisance. I was actually pulled off my flagship restructuring project at work because my team completely lost trust in my decisions. At home, my wife is furious about the money I've burned through and how unreliable and wired I've become.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned being pulled off your project and having relationship issues, but can you tell me if, despite these significant impairments, you've still been able to experience any pleasure or enjoy activities during this past month, or has your ability to enjoy things been affected by your symptoms?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't really thought about it in terms of 'pleasure' or 'enjoyment.' I've just been so incredibly revved up, busy, and focused on my projects that I haven't even stopped to consider how I'm feeling about them. My mind is just constantly running too fast to process things that way."

If they push back at all, I feel this intense, white-hot anger that is completely out of proportion and hard to control.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe the nature of your elevated mood - is it more of an expansive, grandiose feeling where you feel invincible, or is it a more irritable, restless state?\n\nPatient: I actually experience both of those states, swinging from feeling on top of the world like a visionary who is going to reinvent corporate culture, to feeling extremely irritable. When my team pushes back, I snap over minor delays and feel this intense, white-hot anger that is totally out of proportion. Alongside that, my body constantly feels physically revved up and restless, like I can't even sit still on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when these feelings of being 'on top of the world' or 'invincible' occur - are they constant, or do they come and go, and are they related to your excessive activity and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: These 'on top of the world' feelings have been happening for about a month now, ramping up with my project, but they aren't constant since I swing between feeling like a visionary and snapping at my team with intense anger. They are directly tied to my non-stop activity and risky behaviors. When I'm in that high state, I feel driven by a motor to start endless projects and make impulsive, reckless decisions because everything feels so urgent and brilliant in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your mood and energy levels returned to normal, even briefly, since these symptoms started about a month ago, or have they been consistently elevated and unstable?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any moments where things went back to normal; it has been consistently elevated and unstable the entire time. For this past month, I've felt like I'm constantly being driven by a motor I can't switch off. There simply hasn't been a break or any period where I felt like my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if you have experienced any distinct periods of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted for at least one week, or if your mood has been fluctuating rapidly without any clear episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes, this mix of feeling like a visionary on top of the world and experiencing that intense, white-hot irritability has been going on consistently for about a month now. It isn't just rapid, fleeting fluctuations; it has been a steady, ongoing state of instability ever since the restructuring project started.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if there was a specific week or longer period within the past month where you felt consistently elevated or irritable without any significant breaks, helping to differentiate between a manic, hypomanic, or mixed episode?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been the entire month without any breaks at all. Ever since the restructuring project started, I've been stuck in this constant cycle of feeling like a brilliant visionary one moment and then snapping with intense, white-hot anger the next. It has been a solid, continuous block of feeling completely revved up and unstable.\n\nDoctor: You've described a month-long period of elevated mood, increased energy, and impulsivity without any breaks. Can you confirm if, at any point during this month, your symptoms were severe enough to cause significant impairment in your social or occupational functioning, or if they were more of a nuisance but still allowed you to function, albeit poorly?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has absolutely caused severe impairment, far beyond just being a nuisance. I was actually pulled off my flagship restructuring project at work because my team completely lost trust in my decisions. At home, my wife is furious about the money I've burned through and how unreliable and wired I've become.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned being pulled off your project and having relationship issues, but can you tell me if, despite these significant impairments, you've still been able to experience any pleasure or enjoy activities during this past month, or has your ability to enjoy things been affected by your symptoms?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't really thought about it in terms of 'pleasure' or 'enjoyment.' I've just been so incredibly revved up, busy, and focused on my projects that I haven't even stopped to consider how I'm feeling about them. My mind is just constantly running too fast to process things that way.\n\nDoctor: {\n  \"category\": \"differential point\",\n  \"subcategory\": \"episode duration and severity\",\n  \"question\": \"Can you confirm if there was a point"
  }

Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt. Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.
